# HANDOFF — research state as of 2026-10-28

This is the working memory of the project: what has been established, from where, and what is still open. Update it whenever a question is answered. `README.md` is the public summary; this file is the detailed one.

**State on 2026-10-28.** Both titles play on the shared builds, normal `affa0c22` and lean `cd7ea936` (F433).

The second title's launcher:
- runs every compute buffer on the GFX ring (`BC5_DIRECT_COMPUTE_RING=-1`; F412, F416);
- has exp 0341's two guards, the product staircase in the CP check and the host shadow labels (F414);
- has exp 0346 v3 and exp 0343's soft-CP bound (`BC5_DIRECT_HOST_INDIRECT=learn:1100000000 BC5_DIRECT_SOFT_WAIT_MS=8`; F433);
- reads an `input.cfg` with ray tracing on and the system language Polish.

The backend cuts every job over 0xf0000 dwords into up to four IBs of one submit (exp 0349's `ib_split`, on with no switch; F427, F431). It also commits its carried base-1 register only after a successful submit (F424).

On by default from before: the ATRAC9 haptics decoder (F301), the tiler scratch pool (F311), the held tail (F312, ADR 0013), the CS prefix (F321), the SaveData sidecars (F319, F324), the timeout probe (F317, F334), the pad rumble worker (F330), and the live Ray tracing switch (F343, F362).

With RT off the second title runs clean. With RT on, every hang and machine-down of 2026-10-27 followed one dcb that the kernel refused for its size (0xfffff dwords, F431), so a frame was lost (F421–F425, F429). Exp 0349's cut fixes that. On its test build the maintainer played 18 minutes with RT on at 39.2 flips/s, with no hang (F432). Since 2026-10-28 the cut is in both shared builds: the S6N route passed once with RT off and twice with RT on, each RT-on run cutting the route's big frame (F433).

Since 2026-10-27 the box recovers by itself from a GPU death in about 40 s, through the SP5100 hardware watchdog, efi-pstore and bc5-gpu-watch (CLAUDE.md; F417, F420). GPU coredumps are copied to `~/bc5-data/captures/devcoredump/`.

Open (§3):
- the RT-on failures (Q20; F421);
- the night lake's patchwork, still present on the shared build with RT on and off (Q21; F418, F435, F436, F440, F441);
- stray TCP writes (Q22);
- memory in RT-on play: 495 MB available and 2.3 GB of swap a minute before the 19:22 failure (F420);
- the CP check costs ~0.8 ms of GPU a frame with RT on, cheap protection, kept as is (Q23 answered, F439); presentation without a copy (Q18) measured and parked again (F434);
- a host fatal on an AMM page with no mapping ended one RT-on run at 554 s (F438). The ledger build that traps it is exp 0356 (F442), which waits for a test play;
- one S6N RT-on run in ten since the cut hung on a shader write fault, recovered by the ring reset (F443);
- the title's recurring "read 0xc" crash at `eboot.bin+0x3d7eca0` ended the maintainer's fight scene five times. It is the title's frame-block pool, full at frame end, being reclaimed on the never-registered main thread (F444–F448). Trap v3 waits for his play on the test 0358 shortcut (exp 0358, F449).

Experiments run to 0349.

## 1. Thesis

The BC-250 is the same silicon target as the PS5 (Zen 2 + `gfx1013` + 16 GB unified GDDR6). Every existing PS5 runtime targets generic PCs and therefore translates the GPU side (AGC/PM4 → Vulkan, RDNA ISA → SPIR-V). On the BC-250 that translation can be removed: hand the game's command stream and shader binaries to `amdgpu` as-is. Everything else (loader, system libraries, audio, input) is the host: KytyPlus (GPL-2.0), whose system libraries are written independently (HLE) — no PS5 firmware or system-software dump is used, ever (D18, ADR 0012). The console-format command stream is today built by the game's own copies of Sony's `libSceAgc`/`libSceAgcDriver`, LLE-loaded from a game dump's `fakelib/` (D11); an independently written AGC driver in the host is to replace them (Q14). In either form BC5 sits at the `/dev/gc` boundary.

What we deliberately do not do: boot PS5 firmware (PSP/Sony keys/ABL/SMU/hypervisor cannot run on the BC-250; no Sony southbridge, SSD/Kraken, Tempest), load a firmware or system-software dump (D18), write a new emulator, or touch Sony material.

| F1 | PS5 GPU and BC-250 are both `gfx1013` (GFX10.1 + `image_bvh_intersect_ray`), **not** gfx10.3. A Vulkan project for native PS5 homebrew compiles shaders with the ACO GFX1013 backend on a real console (fw 12.02); Mesa/RADV identifies the BC-250 as gfx1013. Consequence: no ISA gap; avoid `gfx10-3-insts`. | mpereiraesaa/ps5-vulkan README; Phoronix "RADV Cyan Skillfish" | High |
| F2 | AGC command buffers are standard PM4 type-3 packets. Public code handles `SET_CONTEXT_REG` (header `0xC0016900` seen in KytyPlus), `SET_SH_REG`, `RELEASE_MEM`, `WRITE_DATA`, `COPY_DATA`, `DMA_DATA`, `DUMP_CONST_RAM`, `DISPATCH_INDIRECT`. astraea frames the AGC DCB as generic PM4 type-3. | astraea-emu/astraea issue #158; boykopovar/AnyPS5 PR #5 (AGC recorder driver); Coder787-source/KytyPlus | High for framing |
| F3 | Sony-specific register usage is expected in draw / CB / DB packets; public AMD opcode tables are not sufficient there. | OpenProspero/AGC README | Medium |
| F4 | `amdgpu` does not parse GFX indirect buffers. User space (RADV, radeonsi) builds PM4 IBs and submits them via `DRM_AMDGPU_CS`; privileged registers are protected by CP firmware. libdrm `tests/amdgpu` contains dispatch and draw tests for gfx10 with embedded shader binaries (`shader_code_gfx10.h`, `basic_tests.c`). | Mesa RADV docs; libdrm `tests/amdgpu` | High |
| F5 | Loading a prebuilt shader binary does not need RADV: the libdrm path writes ISA bytes into a BO and sets `PGM_LO/HI` + `RSRC*` via `SET_SH_REG`. | F6 | *Narrowed 2026-10-15 by F177/F178: the kernel's compute rings run dispatches correctly and the second title's compute buffers run on compute ring 0 (ADR 0009); RADV's own compute queue stays disabled.* BC-250 hazards: RADV disables the gfx1013 compute queue (Mesa MR !33116) — use the GFX ring; a GPU reset can take the machine down (akandr/bc250-rocm logs). | Mesa MR; bc250-rocm | High |
| F7 | CU masks are SH registers written by the submitter: `COMPUTE_STATIC_THREAD_MGMT_SE0-3` (compute; bytes 0xB858/0xB85C/0xB864/0xB868 = SH dwords 0x216/0x217/0x219/0x21A), `CU_EN` fields in `SPI_SHADER_PGM_RSRC3_PS/VS/GS/HS` (graphics; `RSRC3_PS` at mm 0x2c07). Console-side compressors produce `.ffpfsc` directly. A real container matched every reader-relevant field, including the unused block 4 and the PFSC header-size formula. Answers Q6. | pearlxcore/PS5PKGTool @ 0072cb4 (`PS5PKGTool.Ffpfsc/*.cs`); PSBrew/MkPFS @ 78eda0a (`mkpfs/consts.py`, `mkpfs/pfs.py`); psdevwiki PS4/PFS; `experiments/0001-ffpfsc-header-survey` | High (one producer seen; see ffpfsc.md §9) |

Tap point for captures: `DeviceCtl::submitGfxCommand` / `GraphicsPipe::indirectBuffer`. | F15 | A Prosper capture of ASTRO BOT (D10) is **Prosper's command encoding, not the console's**: every draw, dispatch, barrier, flip and marker is a type-3 NOP whose header bits 2..7 carry Prosper's own sub-op enum; only four opcodes occur (NOP, SET_SH_REG, EVENT_WRITE, NUM_INSTANCES). Only the SET_SH_REG (offset, value) pairs are game-derived (0 CU-mask writes). No game shader binaries were captured. Such captures cannot answer Q2/Q4 or feed phase 1; console-format DCBs need Sony's `libSceAgc` under RPCSX (gate G0b). | experiments/0007 | High |
| F16 | HLE hosts need no firmware (Kyty MIT, KytyPS5/KytyPlus GPL-2.0, Prosper unlicensed). On the BC-250, KytyPlus `f266548` does **not** get ASTRO BOT to its first load milestone: stock memory cap → the game aborts on direct-memory exhaustion (it wants ≈ 12.2 GB of direct memory); with a 13.5 GiB pool → deadlock in `PthreadCondWait` before any GPU work (0 command buffers). Prosper reaches the title screen on the same mount. An HLE track on KytyPlus starts with unpriced `libkernel`/fiber work. | experiments/0009 | High |
| F17 | The ASTRO BOT dump ships **its own copies of five Sony runtime libraries** in `fakelib/`: `libSceAgc.sprx` (95 KB text), `libSceAgcDriver.sprx`, `libSceAmpr.sprx`, `libScePlayGo.sprx`, `libScePsml.sprx`, as decrypted SELFs with plaintext code. So Sony's DCB encoder is available from a game dump, without a firmware dump. Candidate third route: HLE kernel + LLE `libSceAgc` from the dump + BC5 backend at the `/dev/gc` boundary. Tested in 0010 → F18. | experiments/0009; KytyPlus `src/loader/runtimeLinker.cpp:2062-2076` @ `f266548` | High |
| F18 | With `fakelib/` as KytyPlus's LLE pack dir, **Sony's `libSceAgc`/`libSceAgcDriver` load, relocate and run** in an HLE host; the game calls into them within 30 s. The two modules need **29 imports** the host lacks; the device half is `ioctl`/`_ioctl`, `mmap`, `kevent`, `sceKernelMapNamedSystemFlexibleMemory`, tool memory (`sceKernelAllocate/Map/ReleaseToolMemory`), `sceVideoOutSubmitEopFlip`, `sceVideoOutGetBufferLabelAddress`, `sceVideoOutSysGetBus`; the rest are process/app-info queries and sysmodule/RegMgr lookups. The fault is `libSceAgcDriver+0x66ac` writing through a NULL driver-context pointer (`+0x22c10`) because driver init failed on those stubs. So the `/dev/gc` ioctl semantics (Q2) can be studied from the driver binary in the maintainer's own dump, without firmware. | experiments/0010 | High |
| F19 | **First console-format DCBs captured (gate G1b).** With the 29 imports and a `/dev/gc` of ours in KytyPlus (`backend/kytyplus-patches/0001`), Sony's `libSceAgcDriver` initialises (device open, submit page `mmap` at 0xfe0200000, nine named system-flexible areas from 0xf00000000/0xfe0300000, 56 queue registrations via ioctl `0xc0408121`, `EVFILT_GRAPHICS` events 0x40/0x48/0x46 via `kevent`) and ASTRO BOT's first submission arrives as `0xc0488131` (context control + a 150-dword state preamble IB + a 2-dword IB) followed by `0xc0188132` (a 96-dword frame DCB). Both decode with `bc5-agc`: 42 packets, 21 opcodes, 0 unknown after adding `PREAMBLE_CNTL` 0x4a and `LOAD_UCONFIG_REG_INDEX` 0x64 (AMD PAL). The preamble loads shadowed registers with `LOAD_*_REG`; the frame uses `SQ_THREAD_TRACE_USERDATA_2/3` as markers, gfx10 `ACQUIRE_MEM`, `RELEASE_MEM`, `ATOMIC_MEM`, `COND_EXEC`. KytyPlus's gfx9 interpreter cannot run them (filler-NOP length, gfx10 `ACQUIRE_MEM`); a BC5 sync-only CP is the next step. Answers the rest of Q2. | experiments/0011 | High |
| F20 | **ASTRO BOT renders whole frames through Sony's `libSceAgc` into a BC5 soft CP.** The stall of experiments 0009/0011 was a KytyPlus fiber bug on Linux (`FiberSaveContext` saved the frame pointer as `rip` because of clang's prologue; fixed with naked functions) hidden by a deadlock in its fault handler. Compute work is not submitted through ioctls: the driver registers 56 hardware-style queues (ioctl `0xc0408121`), the mmap'd submit page holds u64 write pointers in dwords indexed by queue slot − 1, the game appends `INDIRECT_BUFFER` packets to 16 KiB rings; a BC5 consumer polls them. With the soft CP (sync packets only, immediate completion) the game runs its loop: 273 DCBs, 296 compute IBs, 45 EOP flips in 150 s, and moves into level loading. | experiments/0012; `backend/kytyplus-patches/0001` | High |
| F21 | From 718 captured buffers (137,181 packets, 34 opcodes, 0 unknown): compute preambles write `COMPUTE_STATIC_THREAD_MGMT_SE0..3 = 0xffffffff` (all four SEs on a two-SE chip) together with an unresolved SH register mm 0x2e80 = 0; graphics buffers write no CU mask (Q3). Opcode 0x8e is PAL's `IT_GET_LOD_STATS__APU103` (PS5-class APU only) and 0x93 `WAIT_REG_MEM64` (Q4). Heaviest writes: `SQ_THREAD_TRACE_USERDATA_2/3` markers, `COMPUTE_USER_DATA_*`, `SPI_SHADER_USER_DATA_PS_*`. | experiments/0012 | High |
| F22 | **A captured compute program runs natively.** ASTRO BOT's most-used compute program (1,503 of 2,213 dispatches in 150 s) is byte-for-byte IGT's gfx10 buffer-clear shader; Sony's library dispatches it with `COMPUTE_PGM_RSRC1 = 0x402c0041` (bit 30 `WGP_MODE` + bits 21–23; IGT `0x000c0041`) and V# word 3 `0x0004bfac` (IGT `0x1104bfac`: no `RESOURCE_LEVEL`). `dispatch-min` runs the captured bytes with those console values (up to 12,288 groups, the game's fill patterns) bit-exact against the CPU reference. All 20 captured programs disassemble as gfx1013 with LLVM. | experiments/0013 | High |
| F23 | **1:1 mapping works (Q1).** `amdgpu_create_bo_from_user_mem` over anonymous CPU memory + `amdgpu_bo_va_op(MAP)` at the CPU address succeeds on the BC-250 (user VA range 0x10000..0x800000000000): 64 MiB — BO 1.7 ms, map 0.3 ms, GPU fill 0.6 ms (≈110 GB/s), CPU reads the result through the original pointer; 512 MiB — map 114 ms, first fill 58 ms (page-table population), then 3.6 ms. Zero copies. | experiments/0014 | High |
| F24 | **Writable userptr needs anonymous pages.** `DRM_AMDGPU_GEM_USERPTR` over a `MAP_SHARED` memfd mapping fails with `EPERM` (libdrm's helper, which adds `ANONONLY`, and the raw ioctl with `REGISTER\|VALIDATE`, `REGISTER` or `VALIDATE` alone: `EACCES`). KytyPlus backs guest memory with a memfd plus a writable alias, so the direct mode adds `KYTY_BC5_ANON_BACKING=1` (anonymous views; ASTRO BOT runs unchanged under the soft CP with it: 190 submits, 31 flips in 90 s). | experiments/0014 (`userptr-min --memfd`), 0016 `TODO(verify)`: part of the raw-ioctl EPERM may have come from libdrm's `amdgpu_bo_import(kms)`, which refuses raw GEM handles (found 2026-09-30, step d); the kernel itself accepts `READONLY|ANONONLY|REGISTER|VALIDATE` over r-- anonymous pages. | High |
| F25 | **An IB rewritten by the CPU must be mapped uncached for the GPU.** A GTT command buffer fetched once by the CP and then rewritten from the CPU is served from stale GL2 lines on the next fetch (CPU-side coherence does not invalidate the GPU L2): a small first IB left "NOPs then zeros" in the L2, the next IB executed the zeros (type-0 packets) and hung the GPU; on the BC-250 the reset takes the machine down. Nine resets to isolate (KytyPlus host, standalone `Device`, every configuration), fixed by `AMDGPU_VM_MTYPE_UC` on the scratch mapping: the same sequence then passes (`direct-selftest`: 8 NOPs, 152 NOPs, 4 × CONTEXT_CONTROL variants, the recorded console preamble). Single-submission tools (`dispatch-min`) and tools that trash the L2 between submits (`userptr-min`) never showed it. | experiments/0016 | High |
| F26 | **1:1 userptr mappings must follow the guest's VMAs.** With the console's submit-header IBs going through the GFX ring from inside the track-B host (30 OK, 2–11 ms each, no reset), the first guest `munmap`/re-protection under a mapped range turned every later `amdgpu_cs_submit` into `EFAULT` (`amdgpu_ttm_tt_get_user_pages` → `hmm_range_fault` for the stale BO): one BO on the list whose pages are no longer inside an rw anonymous VMA fails the whole submission, and the kernel never touches the ring. The mapping sync therefore revalidates every mapping against `/proc/self/maps` each pass, unmaps the ones that fell out, and an `EFAULT` at submit forces a resync and one retry. Answers the "MMU-notifier behaviour on `munmap`" remainder of Q1: the notifier invalidates the BO, the next CS reports `EFAULT`; it does not hang. | experiments/0016 (step b1) | High |
| F27 | **`CLEAR_STATE` with a non-zero cmd stops the BC-250's CP.** The console driver emits `CLEAR_STATE` (0x12) with cmd 1 and 2 in every frame DCB (under a `COND_EXEC` whose flag the driver raises with `ATOMIC_MEM` after the first frames); AMD's drivers and PAL only ever emit cmd 0. Once one executed on the GFX ring, the CP completed that submission and then stopped 5–100 ms later on whatever came next, even an IB of pure NOPs — four resets and two piecewise runs with NOP probes to isolate. With `CLEAR_STATE` dropped, the game's frame DCBs (draws, waits, atomics and `LOAD_*_INDEX` NOP-ed) run on the GPU: 59 of 59 in 90 s. The rest of the console stream needed nothing else so far: `EVENT_WRITE`, `RELEASE_MEM` with the GPU clock, `ACQUIRE_MEM` with `gcr_cntl`, `SET_BASE`, `SET_UCONFIG_REG_INDEX`, `INDEX_BASE`, `INDEX_BUFFER_SIZE`, `NUM_INSTANCES` all pass. `TODO(verify)`: what cmd 1/2 mean on Sony's firmware and whether cmd 0 is the right substitute. | experiments/0016 (runs 16–20) | High |
| F28 | **A hang inside shader execution is recoverable on the BC-250; a CP-firmware hang is not.** Kernel 7.2.4 answered a GFX page fault under a draw (SQC fetch from an unmapped page) with `ring gfx_0.0.0 timeout` → `Ring gfx_0.0.0 reset succeeded` → `device wedged, but no recovery needed`; the machine, the desktop and the host process survived (the host is wedged for the rest of the run and falls back to its soft CP). The `CLEAR_STATE` hangs (F27) and the stale-L2 hangs (F25) stopped the CP itself and ended in the full GPU reset that takes the box down. So the fatal class is "CP stops executing packets"; shader-side faults cost a run, not a power cycle. | experiments/0016 (run 27) | High |
| F29 | **The game's render targets are not CPU-resident, and the game maps 12.4 GB of direct memory.** Of 55 CB/DB base addresses in ASTRO BOT's context-register tables, 52 had no resident pages (GPU-written only); mapping the guest's ranges in full materialises the 12.4 GB the game maps (a userptr BO is populated completely at CS time) and does not fit the 14 GB box. The direct mode therefore maps resident runs plus windows around the CB/DB bases it finds in each submit's `SET_CONTEXT_REG`/`LOAD_CONTEXT_REG_INDEX` state (hint mapper, 32 MiB per base, +313 MiB for 30 s of the title screen), and the executable's `r-x`/`r--` segments (shader code, rodata) as read-only userptrs. Sizing the windows from `CB_COLOR*_ATTRIB2`/`DB_DEPTH_SIZE_XY` is the next refinement. | experiments/0016 (runs 23–27) | High |
| F30 | **Frames with draws run on the BC-250 from the track-B host.** With `CLEAR_STATE` dropped, waits filtered to self-satisfied ones, the console's register tables loaded by the CP, render-target windows mapped from the context state and the executable's pages kept writable, ASTRO BOT's frame DCBs — draws and dispatches included — execute on the GFX ring (runs 32–33: 91 and 68 frame DCBs, 106 of 107 submits). The remaining faults are texture fetches (TCP) from GPU-generated surfaces the CPU never touched; each is read back through `AMDGPU_INFO_GPUVM_FAULT`, saved as a 96 MiB region (`~/bc5-work/direct-learned.txt`) and mapped from the start of the next run, and the host reopens the device after the kernel's ring reset and continues — one new fault per run so far. No image yet: the presenter does not see the GPU's flip buffer (F21 open item). | experiments/0016 (runs 28–33) | High |
| F31 | *Corrected 2026-10-15 by F177: the hang was our CONTEXT_CONTROL on a compute ring.* **The BC-250's compute rings hang the machine.** The kernel exposes four MEC rings (`COMPUTE 10.0`, rings 0xf, `comp_1.0.0`–`comp_1.3.0`), but the first trivial dispatch submitted to `comp_1.0.0` (`dispatch-min --ip compute --ring 0`, the buffer-fill shader that passes on the GFX ring) took the box down with a full GPU reset. RADV's refusal to use them on gfx1013 (F6) is therefore the rule for BC5 too: every queue of the console — graphics and its compute queues — is serialised on the one GFX ring. Cross-queue waits (`WAIT_REG_MEM64` on labels the other queue writes) cannot be honoured by the CP there without deadlock; the host splits the IB at such a wait, satisfies it on the CPU (the label is guest memory) and submits the rest. | experiments/0016 (run 43) | High |
| F32 | **The scene is drawn through indirect draws fed by GDS ordered-append counters.** ASTRO BOT's compute queues count objects with GDS (real `DMA_DATA` fills and read-backs of GDS, `ds_*` in the shaders); the frame DCB then issues `DRAW_INDEX_INDIRECT`/`DISPATCH_INDIRECT` from those counts. Without a GDS partition the counts are zero and nothing is drawn (empty flip buffers, no hang); with a partition the counts are garbage (x = 1.9e9 …) and an indirect packet keeps the CP busy past the kernel's 10 s (full reset). With the host NOP-ing indirect packets whose arguments are absurd, the game runs stall-free (232 of 234 submits) but still draws nothing. The console's ordered-append state is the missing piece: the 488 captured compute programs contain 886 `ds_ordered_count` instructions (DS opcode 62, gfx10 encoding) plus GDS-flagged DS atomics — the counters are ordered-append counters whose initialisation and wave ordering the console's system sets up in GDS/OA state that a bare allocation does not carry. | experiments/0016 (runs 53–55) | High |
| F33 | **The game's draws do rasterise on the BC-250; the first frames are a handful of full-screen passes.** (Corrected: runs 61–63 read single-valued colour targets as "no draw rasterises".) `SAMPLE_PIPELINESTAT` around every frame DCB: 47 IA vertices, 5 primitives through the clipper and 2,073,600 or 3,326,976 PS invocations per frame (1920×1080, plus 1152×1088), ~6.7 M CS invocations. The flip pass's vertex buffer is a sound full-screen triangle, resident from the first submission. The display buffers still sample zero; whether the flip pass writes black or nothing is open (run 73). | experiments/0016 (runs 70–72) | High |
| F34 | **Two unresolved waits held every frame for 4 s and fed it stale compute results.** (1) The flip label (`sceVideoOutGetBufferLabelAddress` memory): the frame's last `RELEASE_MEM` sets it, the next frame into that buffer waits for 0, and the console's VideoOut clears it at the flip — the host has to. (2) The frame's compute IB and the frame DCB hand over through two labels (compute: dispatches, write L1, wait L2, signals; DCB: wait L1, …, write L2): a host that submits each IB whole, after its waits, deadlocks them until a timeout. The compute IB is split at the wait when its tail only signals. The stream's 64-bit wait is opcode 0x93 (flags, address, 64-bit reference, 64-bit mask, poll interval). Also: `mincore()` calls a swapped-out page not resident (zram on the box) — the mapper takes `/proc/self/pagemap` bit 62 as well. | experiments/0016 (run 72) | High (observed); the fixes' effect on the GPU: run 73 |

So 40 CUs = bits 0–19 × 2 engines, and a 36-CU compute mask is 0x0003ffff. Experiment 0016's "36/40" rows (0x01ff01ff / 0x03ff03ff) were 26 and 28 CUs. | F56 | **The GPU clock: the console runs at or near its cap whatever the load; the dev box's governor does the opposite.** Public description of the PS5 (Cerny, "Road to PS5", as reported): a fixed power budget, CPU and GPU frequency set by an algorithm from their activity rather than from die temperature, capped at 3.5 GHz and 2.23 GHz, expected to sit at or close to the caps most of the time; not under a retail game's control. The dev box runs `cyan-skillfish-governor-smu` with the documented defaults: 1000–1850 MHz, and a load target of 0.50–0.65 — it raises the clock only until the GPU is idle a third of the time. With a frame of 21–24 ms and 15–17 ms of GPU work the load is about 0.68, so the clock during the runs so far was probably below 1850 MHz and the measured GPU times are longer than the silicon needs; the actual clock has not been sampled (`TODO(verify)`: hwmon `freq1_input` during a run). The BC-250 documentation gives 2000 MHz at 1000 mV as a safe starting point and 2230 MHz at 1060 mV as a conservative air-cooled maximum. | F64 | **The water in the second level is two cycles between the game's queues at once, and the host's model of cross-queue waits cannot express them.** Entering water the frame became 4 s (runs 118–119: the main buffer's two CPU-side waits, at 115 and ~11,000, each timing out at 2 s). The compute buffer grows from 3,624 to 3,825 dwords: past its wait it carries the fluid simulation's dispatches and a second wait, so the tail-only split of ADR 0005 (xviii) no longer applies and the whole buffer waits for the slot label the next main buffer sets — while that main buffer waits for the compute head. Splitting the main buffer the same way faults from the first frames (run 123, `0x800000005000`: run 44's objection, met again; pieces are opt-in). **Stopgap in place (run 125): a game-thread wait has 8 ms (`BC5_DIRECT_DCB_WAIT_MS`), and a wait whose queue is blocked on a label this buffer writes, while that queue's buffer writes the awaited label past its own wait, is not waited for.** 859 s, 847,332 submissions, none failed, no timeout, no fault; water and geysers at 40–51 fps, the fluid a frame late. Both fixed, with tests built from the format; the character model the title's animation job needs then loads (the null read at +0x3aa0cb7). (3) The title's ATRAC9 configuration has channel configuration index 7; LibAtrac9's table has six entries and `InitFrame` wrote past its channel array; indices 6–7 are now rejected before the library. Every file of the package decodes through the mount (196 s). Ruled out on the way: fixed maps over live memory, AMM aliasing, the AMPR event filter, the wait compare codes (now AnyPS5's table). AnyPS5 (GPL-2.0, f145139) has a fuller AMM command set and synchronous APR execution; its "90 %" is system-library function coverage, not games. | `experiments/0036-pkg-title-first-screen`; AnyPS5 f145139 `Apr.cpp` (compare table); prosper notes (docs only) | High (one title) |

Backend `Device::map_alias` / host `BC5_DIRECT_ALIASES=1` map the program region again at (0x80 << 40) + address: no fault, but hangs at 124–153 s (runs 251–253); after two of them the reset ended in a machine reset (firmware: "a parity error occurred"), while timeouts with a page fault recover. | `experiments/0056-address-byte-aliases`; runs 251–253 | High for the pointers; the fix open |

Dumped (run 256): 375 packets, 23 `DRAW_INDIRECT` + one 19-draw `DRAW_INDIRECT_MULTI`, state loaded by `LOAD_*_REG_INDEX` tables; every indirect draw names SH +0x280 (mm 0x2e80, one past Mesa's `COMPUTE_NOWHERE`) as its base-vertex/start-instance register — the PS5 driver's sink, TODO(verify). With them (`=multi`) every run hangs at ~150 s (261, 264–268); the GPU ring resets recovered each time. The hung buffers are the 922/942-dword graphics buffers that carry compute work (physics, ray-traced reflections; no draws), submitted from the `bc5-ring` thread. umr through a root-owned read-only script (`/usr/local/bin/bc5-gpu-hang-dump`, sudoers) works under load (waves, PC, SGPRs), but dumps taken at the host's timeout found an idle, fully signaled ring because the kernel's own ~2 s ring timeout had reset it first (run 267: kernel 10810.003 s, host 10810.05 s); the conclusions drawn from them ("no waves", "the job never reached the ring", "a lost fence") were artefacts. The kernel writes `/sys/class/drm/card1/device/devcoredump/data` before the reset (readable via sudoers). Also checked: the game's `PIXEL_PIPE_STAT_CONTROL` mask (16 RBs) matches the board (`enabled_rb_pipes_mask` 0xffff); AnyPS5 (`Execution/src/Pm4.cpp:69-74`, f145139) names SH +0x280 the PS5 driver's "write nowhere" register; prosper (`src/gpu/execute/gpu_executor.cpp:4789`, 1c93ab8) treats tag bits above the usable GPU VA in AGC user data as canonicalized and probes 48- then 40-bit addresses; neither handles jumps through function pointers. | runs 255–270; exp. 0058 (in progress) | High for the rendering and the dump timing; the multi-draw hang open |

The kernel's devcoredump agrees (GRBM SPI_BUSY/CP_BUSY; CP_STALLED_STAT2 EOPD_FIFO_NEEDS_SC_EOP_DONE; runs 270–271). Dumps triggered by the host's timeout come after the kernel's ~2 s ring reset and were misleading (F112). Snapshots of it before the hang (`BC5_DIRECT_SNAP`, run 276): entry 32 (byte 0x100) holds the child link 0x105 — offset 0x100, type 5 — i.e. the node at 0x100 is its own child; the traversal (F113) never leaves it. No CP packet writes the trees; the only programs given their addresses in user data are an empty one (0x90985f200) and a copy kernel that reads them (0x11d0287900). The object tree's descriptor s[56:59] is built from its header (+0x58), the walk starts at the record's root link. In the hung waves the object tree is 0x12102af700 (unchanged between configurations, F115) and the lanes circle its top nodes (links 0x185, 0xa5, 0xc5, 0x205): the suspect is an instance record added with the multi-draw packets (characters) whose root link or flags send the walk into the tree somewhere other than its root. | F199 | **The second title's opening plays: its frozen voice was the host's AJM Opus decoder, a stub returning silence.** Voice 0x30 is a 10-s Wwise Opus loop (mono 48 kHz, 480,000 samples, pre-skip 312, 501 packets) that the cinematic's sync clock counts (its wrap at 10.0 s, F198). KytyPlus's `ajm.cpp` carried a stub `AjmOpusDecoder` (one zeroed 960-sample frame per job, the whole input consumed); the ffmpeg one in `libs/ajm/opus_decoder.h` was never included and the bundled FFmpeg prebuilt has no Opus decoder. Three facts of the AJM Opus ABI, from the title's jobs: the input is a run of packets each preceded by its size as a little-endian uint16; Wwise asks for 512 samples a job (fewer than a 960-sample packet), so a decoded packet is handed out across jobs, else the voice runs at 0.43× real time; and the stream sideband's `total_decoded_samples` and the gapless remainder advance per packet decoded, not per byte handed out — otherwise the title never drains the last packet and the loop never ends. On libopus (`libopus-dev` in the `ubuntu` container, `find_library(opus)`, `KYTY_HAS_OPUS`) with those three, the clock runs and wraps (8.33 → 2.85 → 6.55 s), the animation stream advances at ≈ 25–30 fps and the camera moves (runs 621–622). The 4-channel menu music (mapping family 1) decodes through libopus's multistream decoder with the RFC 7845 layouts. The job contract as derived: `docs/formats/ajm-opus.md`. ASTRO BOT with the same binary (run 625, 150 s, asked by the maintainer): intro and title screen as before at 59–60 fps, no error in its log; its first 150 s submit no job through the old split-decode entry the journal covers (ASTRO's sound was not checked by ear). | experiment 0154; `docs/formats/ajm-opus.md`; `src/libs/ajm/opus_decoder.h`, `ajm.cpp`, `src/CMakeLists.txt` in patch 0001 | High (clock, stream position and screenshots; audio not yet heard) |
| F200 | **Every ATRAC9 stream of the second title uses channel configuration 7, which LibAtrac9 cannot decode; the host now plays them as silence.** Configuration `fe 7e 03 f0` (48 kHz, 32-byte frames, 4 per superframe); LibAtrac9's six configurations decode it at none (best 5 consecutive frames over 60 variants, `~/bc5-work/at9test`), and the first frames repeat a 16-byte block. Before, every such voice died at its first job (`CODEC_ERROR|FATAL`); `atrac9_decoder.h` now decodes configurations 6–7 as silence so the voice lives. The opening's one is 95 s long (4,562,432 samples) and starts when the intro loop ends; the menu's are short. Haptic tracks (KytyPS5 calls these layouts "vibration") or audio: open. The public decoders know nothing of indices 6–7 either: FFmpeg's `atrac9dec.c` rejects a block configuration above 5 ("Incorrect block config"), LibAtrac9's `ChannelConfigs` has six entries, vgmstream passes the bytes to LibAtrac9; shadPS4's AJM ignores the init failure and fails at the first decode. The only lead is KytyPS5 b10bd53's name for them. | experiment 0154; FFmpeg `libavcodec/atrac9dec.c`, LibAtrac9 `C/src/tables.c`, vgmstream `src/coding/atrac9_decoder.c`, shadPS4 `src/core/libraries/ajm/ajm_at9.cpp` (read 2026-10-17, as documentation); `src/libs/ajm/atrac9_decoder.h` in patch 0001 | High for the failure; the content open |
| F201 | **The opening now plays through, and about 90 s after New Game the machine goes down in a GPU reset.** The maintainer's own run (2026-10-17 19:40, shortcut without journals): the opening runs with picture and sound, the sound then drifts from the picture, the graphics slow down, speed up for a moment, and the machine hangs. The kernel journal of that boot ends with `amdgpu 0000:01:00.0: Dumping IP State` at 19:43:08 (the GPU recovery path) with no fault, timeout or OOM line before it (the journal was not flushed; `user-1000.journal` was found corrupted on the next boot); the emulator had started at 19:40:26 and the input record's last press (New Game) came 69 s later, so the reset is ~90 s into the opening. The maintainer: the opening is much longer than 90 s, so this is not the entry into gameplay (the 95-s ATRAC9 stream of F200 is one of its voices, not its length). Run 623 (Claude's, the opening left running) went down the same way at ~30 s. The slow-then-fast pattern is the host catching up after stalls, as in F88's page-fault freezes; whether the reset is a page fault, a ring timeout or the gameplay transition is open — the next run needs the direct path's journal written to disk as it goes (`BC5_DIRECT_JOURNAL`) so the last submissions survive the hang. | `journalctl -b -2` on the dev box (boot 18:25–19:43), `~/bc5-work/run624-user-stderr.log`, `route-record.txt`; run 623 `drive623.log` | Medium (one journal line; timing from the input record) |
| F202 | **The second title's menu artefact (F179) is a wrong bake of new text, about 1 layout change in 20, lasting until the next one; fourteen host variables do not change its rate.** The glyph cells of whole rows carry fragments of other UI pictures (the covered scene, the previews), letters go missing, the title's rule and the frosted highlight bar show a strip of picture; the description text and the preview stay clean. It needs new text (ten sweeps over the same items: clean) and persists until the title re-bakes (10 s and more idle, under 2 s while scrolling), which is what experiment 0130's "1 frame in 18" measured. Runs 653–668 along the whole Visuals list (88 screenshots each, 1–9 flagged in every run): the same rate with synchronous submission on one ring, with cross-queue waits never given up, with the snapshot ring serving compute buffers too, with the 12 KiB per-frame copy left in place, with the graphics pieces off or their prefix without CLEAR_STATE, with the emulator on four cores, and with the title's automatic AMM maps zero-filled; no packet but the flip-label ones is dropped as unmapped. Left: what the bake draws read (descriptors, source images) and the title's CPU side; next is the draw journal of a broken bake against a clean one. | experiment 0155 | High |
| F203 | **The second title's "Sony Interactive Entertainment presents" card ran at a few fps because the host held the title's threads inside APR and map calls that the console answers at once.** The card is the loading screen of F166 (on screen t≈3–15 s; the Bink bumper that ends with the PlayStation logo starts when the loading is done, and the title's Bink threads only then). Three host costs, found with gdb and a 50 Hz `/proc` sampler: every APR submission ran its reads on the submitting title thread, and the parked ones on whichever thread next submitted an AMM map, so the archive thread sat in the host for a whole batch holding the title's lock while game-main waited on it; every guest map (about 5,700 small AMM pages while loading) made a synchronous round trip to the kyty-gpu thread with a Vulkan submit and fence wait (`GpuResourceManager::UnmapMemory`), from the thread holding that lock; and each layout change ran a learned-window pass that rebuilt the 7,000 direct views 191 times (20–65 ms a pass, 6 s in the first six seconds). Now APR buffers run on one host worker per priority queue and a submit returns at once (`WaitCommandBuffer` blocks until done; `KYTY_BC5_APR_SYNC=1` restores the old path), the unmap flush is skipped in direct mode (the caches flush themselves when they hold something), and the learned pass pins its views and keeps its mapping list sorted. Frames in t 4–9 s: 21 → 86 (runs 700 → 713), the card's longest frame 1.45 → 0.47 s, steady 30 fps from t≈10 s instead of 14, the menus 4 s earlier. Left: the learned pass still costs 2 s in 151 calls, the full syncs over 7,000 views while the title maps pages, and the hint imports of the first render targets (70–100 ms a frame at t 5 s). | experiment 0156 | High (eight runs) |
| F204 | **The second title's menu artefact (F179, F202) is GPU context state inherited from other processes' work on the GFX ring; a register-state prefix in front of the title's drawing buffers removes it (0 of 88 screenshots flagged in six runs, against 9–27).** A layout change redraws the UI layer (3840×2160 RGBA8 in the AMM window) in one graphics buffer of 40–60 `DRAW_INDEX_OFFSET_2` draws plus partial redraws; every context register comes from `LOAD_CONTEXT_REG_INDEX` pair tables (185 at the start, 2–6 per draw). A broken redraw leaves the layer's element rectangles (text runs, icons, rules, the highlight row) with alpha 0 while the panel between them stays opaque; the scene and the preview show through (the 4–19 % transparent pixels of the list area in 160 layer dumps a run, exactly at the flagged screenshots, persisting until the next redraw). Everything in the title's own packets and memory is identical between a broken redraw and a clean one and intact at the fence: the texture descriptors, the three atlas surfaces, the blend and colour state the packets set, the vertices, indices, descriptor tables, paint constants and register tables (0 of 162 buffers with a range rewritten in flight; completion 0–20 ms after submission), the raw packet sequence; pieces off, given-up waits and the vertex ring's rewrites were ruled out by a run each; an identical redraw 120 ms after a harmless one broke. The title sets the RB+ blend-optimisation registers (`SX_PS_DOWNCONVERT`..`SX_MRT7_BLEND_OPT`, ctx 0x1d5–0x1df) to 0 once and never in a redraw; plasmashell, Xwayland and the on-screen keyboard use the GFX ring during a run and RADV programs those registers per pipeline, so whatever ran on the ring since the title's last submission is the state its draws inherit. Fix in the KytyPlus patch (`BC5_DIRECT_PREFIX_ALL`, default `draws`): the pieces' full-state prefix (CLEAR_STATE, then every tracked register) before every graphics buffer that draws, 43.9 fps against 45.0 (run 690); the eleven SX registers alone (`sx`) suffice at no cost (run 689); on every graphics buffer the prefix halves the frame rate (run 684). The prefixed-submission path now carries the GPU-executed marks to the title's own buffer: without them the soft CP served that buffer's waits itself, which had cost 7 fps (runs 684–688) — the pieces' path had the same cost at every split. The rate of the artefact grows with the host's load (3 → 27 of 88 as the diagnostics grew). ASTRO BOT on this build (run 626, 150 s, title screen, the default prefix): 59.0 fps at +40 s and 56.1 at +140 s against 59.0/60.0 on the previous build (run 625), clean exit; its first level is still to be measured at the pad (46–60 before; `BC5_DIRECT_PREFIX_ALL=sx` is the fallback). | experiment 0157; runs 625–626 | High |
| F205 | **Deleting a save in the second title stuck on "DELETING SAVE DATA" because the host's save-data dialog stand-in reported the title's progress dialog finished at once; the title reopened it forever.** The title opens `SaveDataDialog` in progress-bar mode (5, display type 3 = delete), deletes while it runs, closes it itself and polls for finished; `Open` setting status finished made the "Save Thread" loop in Open/UpdateStatus (gdb, run 631) and `SaveDataDelete` was never called; the title stops drawing its menu while a system dialog is up, hence the frozen picture with live input. `dialog.cpp` now reports running after `Open`, keeps a progress-bar dialog running until `Close`, and ends a message dialog at the first `UpdateStatus` (as OK); the same for `MsgDialog`. Then the slot came back at the next start: `SaveDataDelete`'s `File::DeleteDirectory` is `rmdir(2)`; it now removes the tree (`std::filesystem::remove_all`). Run 633: both directories of the slot gone, the list shows the slot empty. Also seen: ✕ on a slot then *Continue* loads the game to a black screen — the F201 path, not a save defect. `KYTY_BC5_SAVE_TRACE=1` now journals the dialog stand-ins too. Of the other dialog stand-ins, `LoginDialog`, `SigninDialog` and `ErrorDialog` already ran until the first poll; the web-browser and invitation dialogs (`libWebBrowserDialog.cpp`, `libInvitationDialog.cpp`) answered status 0 to every call and got the same machine (a title opening the browser from a menu, as this one's "upgrade" item would, polls that status). | experiment 0158; `src/libs/dialog.cpp`, `libSaveData.cpp` in patch 0001 | High |
| F206 | **F201's "hang 90 s into the opening" was the host leaking the AMM automatic pool.** With the kernel log, the host log and the direct journal streamed to the laptop (so a hang could not eat them), run 634 ended not in a reset but in the host's own exit "AMM virtual-memory unmap is unsupported: addr=0x807b0b0000": the APR read worker's `memcpy` of a texture (`tex_road`, `tex_environment`) into an AMM page the title had never mapped (gdb on the fault hook, runs 635–636; the same with `KYTY_BC5_APR_SYNC=1`, so not exp 0156's ordering). The title submits texture maps in map-only buffers of ~40 maps; only the first ran: `AllocateAmmAutoDirectMemory` was a bump pointer over the 3 GiB the title gave and then the host's direct memory, with nothing returned on unmap (13,973 maps against 3,575 unmaps in six minutes); once both were gone a map returned `EAGAIN` without a trace, the buffer's remaining maps were dropped, and the reads that followed hit reserved pages. The growth is also the 12 GiB pinned, the 7-s frames and the learned-window rebuilds the maintainer saw as slow-down. `libAmpr.cpp` now keeps the pool as a coalescing free list with first-fit allocation and a map of what each automatic mapping holds; an unmap, or a map replacing part of an old one, returns its pages; a refused map and a failed buffer are journaled. Run 637: ten minutes, 13,779 maps / 3,741 unmaps, no fault, load 2–4 instead of 8–19. | experiment 0159; `src/libs/libAmpr.cpp` in patch 0001 | High |
| F207 | **The opening then stalls about 20 s in (journal t ≈ 140 s): one 12.6–13.9k-dword graphics buffer of the night scene faults the GPU, and the host's 2-s fence timeout plus the 12-s reopen (`BC5_DIRECT_REOPEN`) turn the rest of the opening into a crawl; on the maintainer's runs the kernel's reset took the machine down instead.** The kernel: `[gfxhub] page fault … in page starting at address 0x8000 / 0x11000 / 0x1a000 / 0x2e000` (pages of the first 188 KiB), the host's fault status 0x00600631 = client 3 (GE2, the geometry engine's index fetch) reading — an index buffer at base 0, of a size that matches the title's largest indirect draw (94,322 × 2 bytes = 0x2e0e4). The buffer (`BC5_DIRECT_IB_DUMP=12000:14500:135:14`, run 643, decoded with `bc5-agc`) differs from its successful neighbours by 8 `DRAW_INDEX_INDIRECT_MULTI` (`INDEX_BASE` 0x1210000000, size 0xffffffff, args from `SET_BASE` 1) and 21 `DISPATCH_DIRECT` + 5 `DISPATCH_INDIRECT` in a graphics buffer, and restores state with `LOAD_*_REG_INDEX` after a mid-buffer `CLEAR_STATE`. Not the exp 0157 prefix (`BC5_DIRECT_PREFIX_ALL=sx`, run 642: same fault). The umr wave dump at the timeout finds no waves (the fault has already stopped them). The packets the host drops in that buffer are benign (thread-trace markers 0xc342/3, the always-zero SH register 0x2e80, indirect dispatches whose arguments are not mapped). Dropping the multi-draws (0x2c, 0x38; runs 645–646) changes nothing; **dropping the compute dispatches of the graphics buffers (0x15, 0x16; run 647) removes the fault for the whole run** — a compute shader of that buffer writes what the geometry engine then fetches. The kernel's device coredump names the faulting unit: `WD_UTCL1_STATUS` 0x101 (the work distributor's address translation) with `GDS_VM_PROTECTION_FAULT` 0x200513. The buffer's 26 dispatches run 22 programs (the host now tracks the program and user data through `LOAD_SH_REG_INDEX` tables too, `BC5_DIRECT_DISPATCH_JOURNAL=1` lists them); two of them (0x11d0409b00, 0x11d03aa800) do `ds_append … gds` with M0 from user data 14, and the buffer fills 32 bytes of GDS with `DMA_DATA` and reads a GDS counter back into the `DISPATCH_INDIRECT` argument buffer (0x11d065f200): the console's ordered-append → indirect-dispatch pattern of F32, on the host's real 64 KiB GDS partition (`BC5_DIRECT_GDS_ALL=1`, no shadow). Skipping those two programs did not remove the fault (runs 721–722), and the bisection by program is not yet trustworthy (`BC5_DIRECT_SKIP_PGM` dropped only 3 direct dispatches in 10 s). Next: bisect the 22 programs with the tally as the check, or compare the GDS counter the title reads back against what the shaders appended. The launchers (`play-pkg.sh`, `play-pkg-log.sh`) now keep an inherited `BC5_DIRECT_SKIP_PGM`. *Bisection (runs 724–731, 2026-10-18 afternoon):* skipping all 22 programs of that buffer changes nothing (726, 728); dropping every `DISPATCH_INDIRECT` in every buffer removes the fault (729), and so does skipping the engine's eleven indirect-dispatch programs by id — 0x90997a500, 0x909977100, 0x909973900/b00/d00/f00, 0x909974200/400, 0x909977e00, 0x909936c00, 0x90992af00 (730, with kernel page faults still logged but no fence timeout). Skipping the first six of them took the machine down twice (731: once 5 s after the emulator started, once during the run), so the culprit is in the GPU-driven pipeline those programs form and the remaining split needs the maintainer at the power switch. Four agents read the 22 dumped programs (`scratchpad` disassembly, llvm-mc gfx1013): the writers of draw data among them are 0x11d040c400 (particle quads: `buffer_atomic_add` of 6·N into a DrawIndexedIndirect indexCount that nothing in the buffer resets, plus the vertices), 0x11d041da00 (8 dwords of indirect arguments into a table-fetched V#), 0x11d039c700 (a 128-byte block copy) and 0x11d0288800 (an index/argument replicate), with 0x11d0409b00/0x11d03aa800 appending tile lists through GDS; none of them is the culprit by the bisection. The maintainer's observation of the same state: sound and picture slow down and speed up together while the subtitles keep wall time. *Resolved to one program (runs 732–735):* skipping the five of group B removes the fault (732), three of them (733), and **program 0x909974400 alone (734)**. Its code (dumped from guest memory with gdb, llvm-mc gfx1013, read by an agent): a GFX-ring `DISPATCH_INDIRECT` that links a neighbour graph with `buffer_atomic_or` back-pointers, fills 64-byte records with empty bounds and stream-compacts newly linked element indices behind a byte counter — the engine's BVH neighbour-link compaction (its user data carry the tag `PSR_BVHL`), the stage before the ray-traced-reflection traversal the host already skips (0x11d049aa00, F113–F116). With both skipped the opening plays to its end and the game reaches **gameplay**: run 735, seven minutes, no fence timeout, the machine up, the tutorial prompt "L + △ Leap Attack" waiting for the pad. Both skips are the launchers' default (`play-pkg.sh`, `play-pkg-log.sh`) as diagnostics until the BVH inputs are right (F116, F173): the program reads a reference table and a base from its user data that on the host are zero or stale, so its atomics and the compacted list land where the geometry engine then fetches from address 0 (the agent's reading of the code; the input dump is the next step). | experiment 0159; runs 637–735; the kernel log and device coredump; `bc5-agc`; llvm-mc (gfx1013) on the dumped programs | Medium |
| F208 | **The fault behind F207's skip is the geometry engine reading ~200 KiB from address 0 at the first draws of the night scene, through a base register the console's system programs and the host leaves at 0 — not the compaction's inputs.** With the dispatch journal extended (user data 0–15, indirect base) the compaction's inputs are complete and mapped (BASE_A per object, BASE_B a per-frame ring in the 0x12 heap, the constant block, the `DISPATCH_INDIRECT` arguments at BASE_A+0x40), and the program runs from the menu scene on (journal t ≈ 47 s) without a fault. The kernel's coredumps name the work distributor (`WD_UTCL1_STATUS` 0x101, client GE2) reading pages 0x0–0x2f000 in two rising streams (the two SEs); the timed-out buffer (as the GPU ran it, `BC5_DIRECT_TIMEOUT_PROBE`) has every packet executed, `INDEX_BASE` 0x121a600000 in force (`CP_INDEX_BASE_ADDR` confirms), and its draws are a 3-vertex `DRAW_INDEX_AUTO` with the NGG pass-through shader and one `DRAW_INDEX_INDIRECT_MULTI` with sane arguments; dropping either kind alone leaves the fault, dropping both removes it (runs 745–765). The restore register tables are identical to a run with the compaction skipped, the guest has no memory below 0x40000, and the unknown Sony uconfig writes are constant; re-emitting the index state, masking `VGT_INDEX_TYPE`'s unknown bits, forcing 0xc383 to 0, skipping the buffer's fills/ray-tracing/copy kernels change nothing. The title never calls `GraphicsDriverSetTFRing`; programming the tessellation-factor ring registers to a host buffer moves the same address-0 fault to the first compaction frame (run 770), so the engine's tessellation state is involved but the TF base is not the register read. The skip stays (diagnostic); next is the system-programmed GE buffer (WD index/position/sideband buffers, NGG rings) and what 0x909977100 / 0x90997a500 emit. Tooling: the timeout probe, the packet/table journal, drop-by-offset, uconfig force, the TF-ring prefix (all opt-in). *Continued (runs 780–781):* the three consumers of the compacted list (0x909977e00 leaf builder, 0x909977100 adjacency build, 0x90997a500 bottom-up refit) write only BVH nodes, positions and vertex-index records — no draw/dispatch arguments, index data, ring pointer, GDS word or base (agent readings in exp 0160); with the full-state prefix off (`BC5_DIRECT_PREFIX_ALL=0`) the kernel still logs the address-0 faults but nothing times out and the title plays on, so the GE read is survivable and the hang needs the prefix; with `=sx` the box went down. Next: the WD/GE base registers at the hang via the root hang-dump helper. *Runs 782–784:* not the prefix's CLEAR_STATE (`BC5_DIRECT_PIECES_NO_CLEAR=1` still hangs); the kernel resets the ring 2.0 s after the fault whatever the host's fence timeout, and the root hang-dump helper halts waves before reading registers, so the WD/TF bases it returns (all 0) are post-reset values. *Run 785 (registers before halt_waves, kernel timeout 10 s):* the first GE fault burst is survivable — the dump a second later finds the CP idle and every WD/TF/ES/GS base 0, the title plays on 25 s — and a second burst then hangs a job and the machine resets itself (parity error); the 2-s timeouts were the safe path, so `amdgpu.lockup_timeout=10000` should go. The base cannot be read: those registers are 0 here at every moment. *Runs 786–787:* the learned window at 0 never got its zero pages (amdgpu refuses VA 0, the fill gave up on the piece); retried from 0x10000 the faults shrink to pages 0x0–0xe000 (the kernel's 64 KiB reserved bottom), the stall is caught with the CP busy and every WD/TF/ES/GS base still 0, and the hang is the retried fault itself; `amdgpu.noretry=1` (run 788) changes nothing: 17 timeouts, faults still in the first 64 KiB, a crawl. Closed with the zero pages kept (generic) and the skip kept (diagnostic); the GE base register the console programs remains to be found (root-only writes, or the console driver's init). *2026-10-19 12:19, the maintainer's own play with both skips on:* the same hang 5–6 min into the night scene, faults in the first 64 KiB, the ME at a `DISPATCH_INDIRECT` — the skip only delays it; the faulted range matches the title's indirect-dispatch argument offsets from `SET_BASE` 1, the next thing to test. *12:39 (probe on):* the timed-out graphics buffer has no indexed draw at all (one non-indexed multi-draw, dispatches behind their own SET_BASEs, every packet executed), the faults are four ~40 KiB walks inside the first 64 KiB — not an index fetch, not an argument fetch; the probe now records the per-draw state for the next hang. *13:01 (probe with per-draw state):* the only draw in both timed-out buffers is a one-point non-indexed `DRAW_INDIRECT_MULTI` in GS fast-launch mode (STAGES 0x482030, PRIM_AMP 64, GE_CNTL 0x201, points) — a primitive-shader path no Linux driver uses on gfx10; the state runs from t 44 s without faults, the data (one amplified point) sends the GE to address 0. Dropping such draws by state is too broad (it is the engine's main mesh path: 324k draws in 93 s, the scene missing), so the faulting instance is singled out by its data — its arguments and the work descriptors its ES fetches, the next thing for the probe to read; 13:17: the same draw with 14 records, the probe now dumps the GS user data and the ES's descriptors, and `BC5_DIRECT_FL_JOURNAL=1` journals every such draw at submission for the diff. 13:54 (the fifth hang, with that probe): the address-0 reads are a two-minute stream from t 170 s (ten fault lines per 5-s window, the kernel.s rate limit) during which the title plays on — non-fatal zero reads inside the first 64 KiB — and the 2-s timeouts at t 292 s are the usual pair, the fast-launch draw and the per-frame BVH build buffer on the compute ring (160 trees: 0x909939400, 0x909939700, leaf builder 0x909936c00, refit 0x909939900), the compute one stalled behind the graphics ring (F109). The diff finds nothing in the packet, the arguments or the user data (five V#s, s[20..31] unset, same as 1,233 healthy instances; record count 2 and vertex count 4 are common): the instance differs in what the shaders and the engine read at run time. For the next hang the box has the root hang-dump armed at the first fault (WD/GE registers during the stream) and the hang watcher looping (the stuck ES waves. SGPRs at the stall); the per-draw journal is off again. 14:52 (eight minutes): no GPU hang at all — the "hangs at the transitions" are 1.7 s and 2.1 s stalls of the title.s own thread outside the host (loading), the speed-ups/slow-downs the frame pacing (slow frames: 34 ms of host memory sync, F203/F206); the address-0 stream runs from t 187 s to the end, and the root dump at its first fault finds the CP idle and the WD/TF bases 0 — the read is short and the console-side bases really are 0 here. Caution: the looping `bc5-gpu-hang-watch` (armed 14:17–18:40) halts waves at any 0.6-s stall and each dump was followed by a ring reset — it caused the 17:53–18:03 and run 830/831 resets; arm it only for a dedicated hang hunt. *2026-10-21 (F248, exp 0189):* the address-0 reads are the tessellation-factor fetch through the never-programmed `VGT_TF_MEMORY_BASE`; the title sets its ring through `/dev/gc` (its driver is LLE), and run 770's TF-ring test had a packet bug. | experiment 0160; runs 740–788; the maintainer's 12:19, 12:39 and 13:01 hangs | Medium |

A save is 32 KiB blocks: free = blocks − the files' sizes each rounded up to a block; `GetMountInfo`, the search infos and `GetParam` answer from the sidecars (mtime still the directory's newest write when the title set none, run 573), and `SaveDataDelete` removes them with the save. Frame times in runs 830–838 are not comparable (the CPU sampler, and from 18:00 the Video tab.s RenderScale 1.70 in input.cfg slowed the title 1.5–2× — F237); the hang behaviour does not depend on it. halt_waves dumps only in dedicated runs (F229). | experiment 0160 (evening section); runs 834–838 | High for the loop and its dependence on the compaction; the broken link open |

KytyPlus's `ControllerButtonToPadButton` (`window.cpp`) had no case for it and mapped only BACK, the Create button, to `PAD_BUTTON_TOUCH_PAD`. The recorder writes a button after that mapping, hence its 0 `touchpad` lines in the sessions of 09:33 and 09:56. The native DualSense driver, which maps the touchpad, is off by default and opens on Windows only (F214). Through evdev (`SDL_JOYSTICK_HIDAPI=0`) the pad has no touchpad button at all; the click exists only on the kernel's separate Touchpad device, KDE's pointer (F77). The touchpad button is now mapped by default (`Pad:touchpad=none` drops it); Create keeps its mapping and works as the touchpad button with the current binary. `KYTY_BC5_PAD_TOUCH=1` (off by default) also gives the game up to two touch points (x/y in the 1920 × 943 the host reports, a new id per contact; `TODO(verify)` the console's range and numbering). Mesa's `COMPUTE_PGM_HI` 0x80 and a `COMPUTE_PGM_LO` of 0x50 were left on the BC-250's shared GFX ring, giving 0x800000005000, while the title expects the queue to keep 0x11d039cd00, which it bound at the end of its previous graphics buffer.** Mesa writes `COMPUTE_PGM_HI` once per command stream as `S_00B834_DATA(address32_hi >> 8)` (`src/amd/common/ac_cmdbuf.c:26,64` @ 0866ae7) and then only `COMPUTE_PGM_LO` per dispatch (radeonsi `si_compute.c:401`, RADV `radv_cmd_buffer.c:3264`). RADV's shaders live in libdrm's 32-bit window (`radv_shader.c:1171`; libdrm `amdgpu_vamgr.c:365-367`, `amdgpu_device.c:389-391` @ b97cbde), whose `address32_hi` reads 0xffff8000 on the box. `COMPUTE_PGM_HI` DATA is bits 7:0 (gfx10 register JSON), so window offset 0x5000 is GPU address 0x800000005000, the faulting fetch. Runs 44 and 123 had met such foreign addresses (`bc5LleAgc.cpp:4030, 4166`). On the title's side, all 43 earlier dispatch groups of this program in 78 captured buffers (2026-10-18..21) follow a ten-pair `LOAD_SH_REG_INDEX` binding, one or three packets before them. The 328-dword buffer is the end of the title's usual large dcb (the three dispatches, then its label traffic) behind a fresh dcb opening whose only `LOAD_SH_REG_INDEX` is empty: the probe dumps the buffer before the filter, so the empty tables are the title's own. In session 0933 the same head occurred three times without a fault. The host's carried state named 0x11d039cd00 for those dispatches, although the previous buffer's last dispatch ran another program, so the binding was at that buffer's end. The title also opens draw dcbs with such dispatches (five of 277 captured), but those get the default draw prefix (exp 0157), which restores the tracked program. Only a buffer that draws nothing goes without it. In 0808 that buffer waited 18.8 ms in sync and hints with the title's jobs done, and another client's compute dispatch ran in the gap. Prepared (`patch_0195.diff`, a separate binary `~/bc5-work/exp0195/kyty_emulator`; the shared tree is untouched): `BC5_DIRECT_CS_PREFIX=1` (off by default, D16) puts the graphics queue's tracked compute registers (SH 0x204-0x3ff, as the draw prefix replays them) in front of a buffer that gets no other prefix and dispatches before it sets `COMPUTE_PGM_LO`; `=journal` is the probe that only journals such buffers. `alias_learn` no longer saves an alias for a fault whose low 40 bits are not guest memory (the "0x80 0x0 0x6000000" of F251; `BC5_DIRECT_ALIAS_LEARN_UNMAPPED=1` restores it). Offline, the extracted functions find the 0808 buffer's dispatch at 32, and the queue state tracked after a captured group names 0x11d039cd00, which a 58-dword prefix reproduces exactly (40 registers). Three are "RTV First-Use Init Clear" › ClearRenderTargetView (program 0x11d0288a00, 16 B a thread); one is ClearUnorderedAccessView (0x11d0288800, 4 B a thread). Each clear has one thread per record, so it writes the whole extent. In two cases the same address is then bound as colour target 0: RGBA8 340×150 and 1364×434, SW_64KB_R_X. In the measured session the voice stopped because its next loop's data, a 92.8-KB streamed read made every 10.0 s before, was never read again. That happened at the onset of a cutscene's streaming burst, with the host's APR reads seconds to minutes behind. It was not the memory stalls themselves, the frame rate, the AudioOut2 grains or the AJM ids.** The characters' time is the cinematic player's clip time, read from the running title's memory (`/proc/<pid>/mem`, no attach). game-main stores `GetSourcePlayPosition(id, &ms, false)` × 0.001 at 0x90ae007b4. For a synced main player (+0xc8, +0xc9 == 1, +0x120 & 3 == 3), the clip advance 0x903b77670 runs exp 0153's filter on `dt / *(double*)0x90bee5f48` (1.0), then multiplies by clamp(+0x108, 0.0001, 4), times `(+0x560)->+0x50` when flagged. The kept logs of 1136 and 1345 show the main AudioOut port at 1.000 through their cutscenes. **Seen twice.** L1 (14:47, exp 0199's watched run): the clock stood at 9.993 for at least 84 s, with memory quiet for the first 40 s. S1 (15:14, the maintainer from `saves-pre-1136`, `launch0200.sh S1 log`): it stood at 9.993 from t 320.5 s to the end (~710 s); the port ran at 1.000, a/p = 1 and the game at 37–48 fps. The maintainer heard background, effects and music, but no new sounds. **The stream:** the clock voice's data is entry +0x7c69535a (0x16a83 bytes) of file 0x7cd713df. The APR path read it within 0.1 s of 26 of its 27 pass boundaries up to t 280 s, then late at 293.6 and 301.6 s, then never again; its AJM instance got its last job at 320.44 s, at the end of a full pass, with no failed job. No read failed or came back short. **The burst:** from t 280 s the title submitted 80–125 APR buffers a second (17–42 before). Submit-to-finish rose to a mean of 3–16 s on worker q1 (up to 129 s) and throughput fell from ~70 MiB/s to 1–8 MiB/s. The APR workers were in D state 35–85 % of the time, PSI io full was 26–42 % and MemAvailable 0.55–0.70 GB; the cutscene's `animstrm` (0x628c6db3) went 74 s unread. By t 520 s the queues kept up again, but the clock voice was not requested again. **A separate host defect found on the way, not this freeze's cause:** KytyPlus `ajm.cpp` `AjmInstanceCreate` (f266548; upstream 3eee96a :639–650) reuses an instance id while it is live once its 14-bit counter wraps. Destroying either instance then erases the shared entry, and the other's decodes fail (:421–425). AnyPS5 skips live ids (`libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp:1723–1740` @ 27c88a8). In fights the title makes up to 3,010 instances a minute (exp 0185), and exp 0185's L1 run wrapped at 406 s; S1 reached only 4,848. `patch_0200.diff` adds `KYTY_BC5_AJM_UNIQUE_IDS` (off by default; `=log` journals each reuse, `=1` is AnyPS5's skip), built into a test binary only (`~/bc5-work/exp0200/kyty_emulator`). The probe's 345 lines exist only in the stream: the buffer had run to dword 7,759 of 7,899 (every draw ran or was dropped by the indirect-draw check), so the stall is at its end. Before each of the three: "two rings: … waits at 418 on 0x12004b3c38: NOT served", then "cpu-wait … GIVEN UP" (at dword 379 once). That is a WAIT_REG_MEM64 for the label to equal 0 while it holds 0x907616589. After a give-up the host still submits the buffer, so the GPU waits itself. The give-up is common (68 times in 17 min of the 11:36 session, 16 in 10 min at 16:01, no hang), so three of three within 71 ms is unlikely to be chance (~0.4 % each). 07:44 (reflections on, nothing skipped) does not show it. Every ring timeout that the kernel reset successfully (08:16, 09:55, 10:28, 10:51, 12:01, 12:47) left the box up. Open: who writes and clears 0x12004b3c38, why it is not served in the rare case (a cross-ring deadlock with the compute buffer that timed out alongside is the guess), and why the GFX reset fails (TODO(verify): CP still in the wait). Next: offline, the label's writers; then a journal-only probe of given-up waits; only then a mitigation behind a switch (e.g. the `BC5_DIRECT_SPEC_WAIT_MS` watchdog extended to given-up waits), one watched run (D16). | experiment 0204; sessions play-20261022-1633, -1430, -1337, -0744; kernel journal of 2026-10-22 | High for the 16:36 sequence and for the reset outcome; medium for the precursor's link (3 of 3, base rate measured); open for the label's writer and the mechanism |

No PM4 packet in 202 timeout dumps writes it (exp 0204). The filter drops a WAIT_REG_MEM[64] whose label nothing in its buffer writes unless the host keeps it for the GPU, and it keeps one only when a queued job will satisfy it (`backend/src/policy.cpp`, `self_waits_only`; `ring_will_satisfy` in patch 0001). Most waits on the label are served within the host's 4–8 ms; a few a minute are not. The limit is `BC5_DIRECT_DCB_WAIT_MS` (default 8, set in run 124 for true single-ring cycles); this wait is not one, its writer runs elsewhere. Memory pressure alone does not hang the box: exp 0203's O2 had ~44k swap-ins/s, PSI memory full 10–18 % and a 1-s stall of all threads, and ended normally. Next: the same bench with `BC5_DIRECT_DCB_WAIT_MS=100` (no build) to see the give-ups go and what it costs. In run O2 the read came late twice, once because the title recorded the request ~4 s late during a swap storm and once because the APR queue ran it 2.9 s late; the clock lost ~4 s and recovered. The protocol: the title's stream flush (caller 0x903c7c1ba) records each read in its own command buffer with three completion commands (kernel event eq 1 id 1 with a sequence number, a write of that number to 0x90adf7fe0, a write of 3 to the buffer's status word) and submits it at priority 1. Run O1 (the scripted New Game opening, 600 s) had no stop: the entry +0x7c69535a was recorded and run within a millisecond at every 10-s pass, all 810 sequence writes increasing. Run O2 started from S1's own autosave just before its second lagging cutscene (scripted Continue, 600 s). The clock fell to 0.66–0.80× at t 170–180 s. The entry due at ~178 s was recorded only at 181.8 s, while the box swapped in up to 44k pages a second and every emulator thread got no CPU for about a second; earlier, a record at 158.0 s ran at 160.9 s. q1 submit→finished in t 120–200 s: median 0.15 s, 95th percentile 3.4 s. Which held in S1 (not requested or requested late) cannot be known; S1 ran without the journal. The read-ahead: up to N reader threads per queue (`bc5-apr-r<q>-<k>`) read the files of queued buffers into host memory, capped at `KYTY_BC5_APR_READAHEAD_MIB` (default 32), and the in-order worker copies each read into the guest at its turn, so every guest write keeps the queue's order. Every change that makes a buffer pickable already notifies under `g_apr_queue_mutex`: a submit, and the worker's pop, which frees the 32-MiB budget and moves the 64-buffer window. Frames: 28.7 against 37.1 flips/s, over 100 ms 82 against 43, over 1 s 3 against 4. S1's loading screen lasted about 70 s at PSI io full 40–52 % (S1N about 25 s). Every job number has a `timing: #N done` line, and no job finished inline in the windows, so the in-flight counts are complete. Writer: in 9 of 13 no graphics buffer ran between the give-up and the flip. The flips come 0.6–1.2 ms before an in-flight compute buffer completes (2657, 5508, 5544 dwords), or within 0.2 ms of one (825–865 dwords for 0x12004b5438). Then the compute buffer #107054 was submitted, numbered before the frame's graphics buffer and synced 0.4 ms before it; it ran 0.2 ms, and the label flipped 15 µs after. The compute-side label was therefore written outside the tracked jobs (title CPU code or a host path). These are not cycles, contrary to exp 0055's `DCB_WAIT_UNBLOCKED_MS`, which keeps the short limit for a blocked queue. A rule: keep waiting (bounded) while the waited label is in `g_unready_writes`; give up early only for a real cycle (the compute label written by the waiting buffer; run 123's `cycle broken` covers PM4 writes). In the A/B at the lake bench (4 × 600 s, alternating, the same binary, old launcher values, the 8-ms default, all clean): give-ups 10 and 18 per run off, 0 and 0 on; the two-ring "NOT served" waits 26/30 against 0/0. The game thread spends 1.5 ms less a frame inside the host (label waits −0.8 ms) and 1.8 ms more at the flip, where the held work now goes. So the waits of F284 are released by the title itself once its thread is free. One hold in a load lasted 314.6 ms. That is benign: the 100-ms bound slips while the owner sits in long submits, but at most to the next flip, whose drain waits for it, and the hold ended released. | F322 | *Corrected 2026-10-24 by F332: the one machine-down the synced probe caught is a compute dispatch of the BVH build that never finished, with no wait kept in the compute chain; the graphics buffer waited honestly for that chain's last write. The "missed 0 / cross-ring cycle" mechanism below is refuted for that case and unlikely for these.* **The surviving two-ring timeouts (the 22:59 play, L7, R11B) are one and the same pair. A graphics buffer waits on the GPU for a compute-written hand-off slot. The "Lighting Prep" compute buffer, at its first packet, waits for the graphics-written slot that the next graphics buffer writes. Neither can release the other, so the deadlock must close through the rings' order. With honest registrations that is impossible, so a kept wait must have missed its 0. The TCP faults' writer is still not found.** Exp 0255 decoded the probe dumps offline (`dec0255.py`, exp 0260's decode). **The compute buffer** is the same each time (865/891/875 dwords: the same 8 dispatches and programs, a ClearUnorderedAccessView, a CopyResource). It opens with a WAIT_REG_MEM64 on 0x12004b53c8 == 0 and writes 0x12004b5438 = 0 near its end. F260's and F262's 855- and 875-dword compute buffers were probably this one. **The graphics buffer** (66,622/19,968/31,651 dwords) has F262's WAIT_REG_MEM64 (pfp) on 0x12004b3c38 == 0, and in 22:59 also 4438 and 4c38. **Two hand-off rings:** graphics writes the "…c8" slots (0x400 apart: 4bc8, 4fc8 = 0) that compute waits on; compute writes the "…38" slots (0x800 apart: 3c38 … 5438 = 0) that graphics waits on. Neither stuck buffer writes the other's label. In 22:59 the ring order kept this family's compute waits for the GPU routinely ("acb 1445 dwords keeps its wait at 0 on 0x12004b37c8 for the GPU (296961 so far)"). Graphics waits on 3c38 were kept in two-ring mode (38,970 of 41,985). Two cpu-wait give-ups on 3c38 and 5438 came shortly before the timeout. **Why a missed 0:** the ring order keeps a wait only on a write already submitted, so a cycle through the FIFO order would need A < A' < D < D' < A. So a kept wait missed its 0, either through a stale registration (the job done, `forget_label_writes` not yet run, the slot re-armed) or through the title's CPU re-arming the slot between the GPU's write and the wait's read (F262: 3c38 held 0x907616589; F282/F283). Either way it lies in the ring-order keep of these hand-off waits (exp 0055, ADR 0009). It is not in the held tail or the CS prefix: all three cases predate both. **The TCP faults:** reported only by the host's fault query at the timeout ("timeout: fault 0x2675dfa000"), the window learned afterwards; no pointer into it in either buffer and no executed offsets, so the writer is open. | experiment 0255 (`raw/*-dec.txt`, `raw/*-markers.txt`, `raw/2259-labels-near-timeout.txt`, `raw/cases.txt`) | High for the pair's structure and labels; the missed-0 mechanism is a hypothesis for exp 0260's probe to test |

The other hitches of that session were stalls of the whole process. The lean session's hang and its order glitch have no record.** The host sends a pad-speaker or vibration port to the controller only when an output device's name contains "DualSense" (`KYTY_BC5_PAD_AUDIO_DEVICE`, `audio.cpp` `OpenSdlDevice`). The DualShock 4's own sink, "Sony Interactive Entertainment Wireless Controller" (2 ch, 32 kHz), does not match. So its pad-speaker port opens on the default sink with `pad_route` 0, and only pad-routed ports are never waited for. In the normal build's audio trace the title writes port 0 (type 0, 8 ch) and port 1 (type 4, 1 ch) in lockstep. At t 149–150 s the thread was held 493 and 635 ms in port 1, against 0–8 ms elsewhere. Port 0's queue ran empty (rate 0.835, 0.816). The vibration port (type 10) has no SDL device then. `AudioOutOpen` still returns it, and its blocks go to F214's rumble fallback on the same thread, before the trace counts a port. So its timing is not in the data. Since F301 those blocks carry decoded haptics, so the fallback calls `SDL_GameControllerRumble` on every change and every 40 ms while non-zero. The session's audio clock lost 1.93 s net in about 120 s. The largest single loss, 0.94 s at t 156 s, came with the audio thread making 21 calls in 1.16 s and the game thread inside the host 239–569 ms in single steps: filter 519 ms, sync, hints, label waits. Such steps did not occur in the 16:01 session or in exp 0242's R1 (filter max 19 and 41 ms). That points to stalled threads. Steam ran until 16:08:43 with a logged peak of 4.3 GB of memory; no memory sampler ran. The 16:01 session had the controller swapped mid-run: DualSense out 16:03:07, DualShock 4 in 16:03:51. The lean build records no traces or journal, and its pad recording was overwritten by a later session. The "shared map at 0x0: -22" lines (about 1,000–2,000 a run) predate these sessions. Next: a switch that never blocks the audio thread on a pad port without a controller device. It goes into exp 0267's diff, together with that diff's rumble worker thread and `KYTY_BC5_PAD_SPEAKER=off`. Matching the DualShock 4's sink instead would send the haptics to its speaker. Then an A/B on exp 0203's O2 route with the DualShock 4 attached, also with `KYTY_BC5_PAD_RUMBLE_FALLBACK=0`, with the samplers and the fallback's call times. | experiment 0268 (read-only: play-review-20261024 logs, journals play-20261024-1601/-1605, box journals 15:58–16:12, `audio.cpp`/`controller.cpp` of the shared tree) | High for the pad-speaker hold at t 149–150 s and its code path; medium for "the rest were whole-process stalls"; none for the lean hang and the order glitch (not recorded) |

Second, "unguarded" describes the dump, which is the host's copy before the backend's filter: the backend inserts the CP check after it, and the journal shows the check active ("1919454 dispatches checked by the CP", line 170835, before the timeout). The probe's "ran" means "passed the filter", not CP progress.* **The first hang with probe v2's grids and a saved devcoredump (2026-10-24 18:52, exp 0273's binary with RT on) is F322's pair, and the graphics ring stopped first, in its own work. File refaults reached ~200 k pages per 10 s and flips 12–32/s; he stopped on a slowdown, with no GPU line. | F401 | **The second title crashes when the player's character dies (the maintainer, 2026-10-26 15:06, RT off).** It is a write through a null pointer in the title's code: `and byte [r10+0x26], 0xfe` at `eboot.bin+0x3ba1bdf`, r10 = 0, exit 65. That is a different site from exps 0299/0326's crash (read 0xc at +0x3d7eca0), which also ended an earlier session of his (F397). Both dumps list unnamed NpCppWebApi NIDs among the most recent stubbed imports: XuVY56j7GM0, BY1jyWLZrcA, JiY4sfHkV34 and LbhH9NeB93I here, and four others at +0x3d7eca0. These are among the ten that F393 left with no candidate name, a lead not yet tested. When the emulator left, KDE's screen locker came up; the session itself stayed (not a logout). *Correction 2026-10-26 15:40:* the NpCppWebApi lead is dropped. The crash dump's "recent stubbed import" lines are the last stubs created at link time (`runtimeLinker.cpp`, exp 0327), and the ten unnamed NpCppWebApi NIDs are the only stubs left, so every dump lists them. Neither crashed session (11:08, 14:31) has a `[bc5] stubbed import called` line, so the title never called them. The crash at +0x3ba1bdf is in the title's own logic, and its cause is open. *Update 2026-10-26 16:00 (the code read on the box from the fake-signed SELF's executable segment, as KytyPlus's elf.cpp maps it; no code bytes or disassembly kept):* the crash is in a loop that releases a list of 16-byte handle entries. Each entry gives a table number (16 bits), an element index (32 bits) and a byte that picks one of two table groups. The element comes from a bounds-checked lookup that yields null when the index is at or past the table's count, or negative; the loop then writes through it without a check (`and byte [elem+0x26], 0xfe`, then `[elem+0x10] = 0` and `[elem+0x24] = 0xffff`). At the crash the loop was at its first entry (r9 = 0), naming table 0xcae and element index 0x2064cd3 (~34 M). The list holds garbage, not stale handles of a short table: its memory was overwritten or never filled. Who wrote it is open; F360's GPU store into CPU data is one candidate. *Update 2026-10-26 16:15 (exp 0332):* the crash does not come with every death. On the test shortcut with exp 0332's frame dump, the maintainer's character died several times in the same way in an 11-minute session (15:53–16:05, RT off). There was no crash, and the session ended with his own exit (exit 0). The 15:06 crash is a single event so far, and the frame dump stays on the test shortcut for the next one. | experiment 0331 (`raw/maintainer-test-2/crash-context.txt`); experiment 0332 (`raw/maintainer-test/`) | Medium for the site (one crash); sporadic, not tied to every death; the cause is open |

Kernel log: no amdgpu line; no new devcoredump; 0 `TIMEOUT` lines in both journals. Shadow labels: 3.1 M and 3.2 M in 298 k and 304 k jobs (~10.5 a job), 119/131 without a slot, 0 IBs left alone. 31.7 and 39.1 flips/s. Stalls: a 34.4-s gap in the first game with the game thread outside the host (the title's own load), and in the second at t 864.9→886.9 s the dcb #1197600 (6,866 dwords) and four GFX jobs after it waited 22.0 s for their fences, the game thread 22.04 s at the flip, "soft-CP passes 22,009.5 ms in 469", audio on, the journal silent. With `lockup_timeout` 2 s (F316) a GPU job that ran 22 s would have been reset, so the jobs were held on the host. None of the 13 journals of 10-25…10-27 before it has a frame over 4.1 s. *Cause (the bvh session, from the excerpt and the code):* the hold was in the game thread's completion queue `bc5-cq0`: the soft CP's replays of the buffers before the jobs polled a label 11 times to the hard-coded 2-s deadline of `SoftCp::wait_reg_mem` (22,009.5 ms = 11 × 2,000 + 9.5), while `drain()` held the game thread at the flip; the GPU had run the jobs at once (later ring-thread jobs completed at 864.91 s), the timeline's `fence` is queue-to-dequeue time, and the soft CP's timeout is a `LOGF` line, silent under `--printf-direction Silent`. A replay polls only unmarked waits, so the marks of a 7,332-dword DCB did not reach its replay (empty marks = execute everything); the shadow labels cannot cause it (the marks index the original buffer, the replay runs on it). Proposal: journal soft-CP polls over 50 ms and timeouts, give replays their own bound (8 ms) or no polling with empty marks, then find the lost marks. Aside: the F2 overlay's language list ran up by itself because the left stick's analog value reaches ImGui without a dead zone (only the down state has one), and ImGui's gamepad navigation scrolls by that value every frame. | experiment 0341 (README "The maintainer's RT-on play", `raw/maintainer-test/`) | High for "no GPU hang in 46 min" (kernel log, journals); one play, so not yet a rate; which of the three changes did it is not separated; the 22-s hold's wait is open |

The owner is the thread that last called the owner setter, and it helps through +0x3ccb180 → +0x3cca600. The −1 index of the main thread is by design (F447). The title's code is the same on the console; what can differ is how often the owner has to help at frame end, i.e. the workers' throughput, and what is pending then. Trap v3 (build 1691b436, on the test 0358 shortcut) prints the job system's globals and every pending job at the fault, and samples the entries once a second at the flip.
